# Chapter 1 Sensor Faucet (Tag: tap, 50 Entries)

### Q: Is GIBO's sensor faucet sensing technology independently developed? How does it differ from externally sourced technology? | tap,sensor,company

A: GIBO's sensor faucet sensing technology is independently developed. With 20 years of R&D experience in infrared/microwave sensor modules, the algorithms, mainboards, and structures are all designed in-house. Compared to externally sourced technology, it offers greater stability and stronger anti-interference capability, effectively reducing false triggers and malfunctions, and adapts to various complex environments.

---

### Q: Is GIBO's sensor faucet sensing technology independently developed? What proprietary core technologies does it have? How does it differ from externally sourced technology? | tap,sensor

A: Yes, GIBO possesses independently developed sensing technology. Proprietary core technologies include: Reliability Theory infrared photoelectric sensing technology (strong anti-interference capability), BA/UA power conversion system (automatic soft switching between AC/DC), and sensor modules encapsulated with triple moisture-proof processes.
Compared to generic externally sourced modules, proprietary technology enables deeper optimization of product performance, stability, and power consumption, and allows customized adjustments based on specific customer requirements, forming a core competitive advantage for the product.

---

### Q: Which type of cartridge is best for sensor faucets? What are the advantages of GIBO's cartridges? | tap

A: For sensor faucets, ceramic disc cartridges are the preferred choice. The industry standard is 500,000 on/off cycles without leakage. GIBO sensor faucets come standard with high-quality ceramic disc cartridges across the entire lineup, offering smooth operation, wear resistance, and durability far exceeding industry standards. They effectively prevent cartridge leakage and sticking issues, extending the product's service life.

---

### Q: What is the standard for low-power sensor faucets? What are the advantages of GIBO's low-power products? | tap

A: An industry-qualified low-power sensor faucet must have standby current ≤ 60 μA. To achieve truly low power, it should be below 30 μA. All GIBO sensor faucets meet this standard. Four AA batteries can last 4–12 months, significantly reducing battery replacement frequency and maintenance costs, making them ideal for high-frequency commercial environments without pre-installed power wiring.

---

### Q: What are the advantages of dual-power (battery + mains) sensor faucets? Which scenarios are they suitable for? | tap

A: The core advantage of dual-power sensor faucets is that water supply is not interrupted during a power outage, and installation is more flexible. Even without pre-wired power, mains electricity can be used when available. During a power outage, the system automatically switches to battery power, preventing water stoppage due to power loss. They are ideal for hotels, hospitals, schools, and other commercial environments requiring high water supply stability.

---

### Q: What type of solenoid valve is better for sensor faucets? What are the advantages of GIBO's solenoid valves? | tap,sensor

A: The key to a sensor faucet lies in the reliability and lifespan of the solenoid valve. Pulse solenoid valves offer extremely low power consumption, low heat generation, and longer lifespan compared to continuously energized solenoid valves.
GIBO's solenoid valves are meticulously designed and rigorously tested, with the following advantages: a repair rate below 5‰, indicating extremely high reliability; modular design enabling quick standalone replacement of the valve body in case of failure, with simple maintenance requiring no professional personnel.

---

### Q: How should I choose a sensor faucet? Is there a difference between commercial and residential selection? | tap

A: When selecting a sensor faucet, prioritize full brass body + ceramic disc cartridge, support for battery/mains dual power, standby power ≤ 100 μA, and anti-false-trigger and anti-dry-burn features. Commercial sensor faucets emphasize high-frequency durability, anti-interference, and vandal resistance, suitable for hotels, hospitals, schools, and other public venues. Residential sensor faucets emphasize low power consumption, aesthetics, and easy installation. With 20 years as a sensor faucet manufacturer, GIBO offers a full range of scenario-adapted products to meet different commercial and residential needs.

---

### Q: What are the surface finishing processes for faucet bodies and their characteristics? | tap

A: Common faucet surface finishing processes include chrome plating, PVD, powder coating, brushing, and ORB, each with distinct characteristics:
1. Chrome Plating – The most common finishing method on the market. Chromium is a silvery-white metal with a bluish luster, possessing strong passivation ability. It does not easily change in humid environments and maintains surface gloss for a long time. Its precious-metal-like appearance is highly decorative, making chrome-plated faucets popular among consumers.
2. PVD (Physical Vapor Deposition) – An advanced surface treatment technology widely used internationally. Under vacuum conditions, gas discharge is used to partially ionize gas or evaporated substances, depositing the evaporated material or its reaction products onto the substrate under ion bombardment. PVD offers rich, elegant colors, excellent resistance to harsh environments, easy cleaning, and non-fading performance, making it highly favored by consumers.
3. Powder Coating – Primer and topcoat are applied to the faucet substrate, with each coat cured in a dust-free, constant-temperature baking chamber using electric or far-infrared high-temperature baking. Powder-coated faucets have smooth surfaces with brilliant gloss, presenting a piano-like visual texture that gives the product a premium look.
4. Brushed Finish – Brushed treatment gives the faucet a textured metallic surface appearance.

---

### Q: Can sensor faucets be forced to output water? Do engineering models have emergency functions? | tap

A: GIBO engineering-grade sensor faucets support forced flushing/emergency switch functions, meeting property management and emergency repair needs while preventing water stoppage due to sensor failure. They are suitable for hotels, hospitals, schools, and other commercial environments requiring high water supply stability.

---

### Q: Will dual-power sensor faucets malfunction when the battery runs out? Is there a low-battery reminder function? | tap

A: They will not malfunction. Dual-power models adopt a mains-first, battery-backup mode. When mains power fails, they automatically switch to battery power. Battery-only models are equipped with a low-battery reminder function; when battery power is low, a reminder signal is emitted to prevent sudden water stoppage and ensure normal use.

---

### Q: With so many product functions, is training required to use them? | tap,sensor,soap

A: The 2-in-1 Soap Dispenser Faucet features three functions – instant-sense water output, sustained-sense water output, and foam dispensing – controlled via three sensing windows:
1. Front-sensing instant water function, designed based on ergonomic hand-washing habits, with a dTOF Laser sensor at the spout tip. Water flows upon reaching out and stops automatically upon withdrawal, eliminating self-triggering. No training required.
2. Side-sensing sustained water function, designed for right-hand operation. Water starts with a hand wave and stops with a second wave, with a 3-minute auto-shutoff protection to meet household water-saving needs. No training required.
3. Top-sensing foam dispensing function, based on thorough research of foam dispensing habits, using an optimal dual-sensor anti-false-trigger design with a foam icon prompt. When the hand reaches the top sensing range, the foam icon illuminates and foam sensing activates. Within 3 seconds, placing the hand in front of the front sensor confirms foam dispensing; if the hand is not placed in front within 3 seconds, no foam is dispensed. Simple to use, matching the habits of most users.

---

### Q: Does the temperature display indicate a heating function? What is its accuracy? | tap

A: The sensor faucet does not have a heating function. The product uses cold + hot water dual-inlet mixing for output, requiring a water heater to produce hot water. The LED digital display only serves as a temperature indicator, with a digital temperature accuracy of ±1°C.

---

### Q: Is there an auto-shutoff function? | tap

A: Yes, multiple layers of automatic timed shutoff are built in, along with power-off protection.
Front sensor: 1-minute timed shutoff. Side sensor: 3-minute automatic timed shutoff. Battery-powered 4.6V low-voltage auto-shutoff protection. AC-powered models automatically shut off water during power outages, ensuring safer use.

---

### Q: What materials are used for the faucet body and water channels? Do they meet health standards? | tap

A: The faucet body is made of zinc alloy (Zn) with electroplating, capable of passing 24-hour acid salt spray tests or 200-hour neutral salt spray tests.
Internal water channels use NBR hose + PA66, free from lead (Pb), cadmium (Cd), mercury (Hg), hexavalent chromium (Cr6+), polybrominated biphenyls (PBBs), and polybrominated diphenyl ethers (PBDEs). Compliant with RoHS and NSF standards, suitable for healthy drinking water use.

### Q: The sensor faucet is not dispensing water – how to quickly troubleshoot? | tap,trouble

A: Prioritize the following 5-step self-check:
1. Battery depleted: Replace with 4 new AA alkaline batteries (recommended brands: Duracell, Energizer, etc.).
2. Sensor window dirty/obstructed by water stains: Wipe the sensor window with a soft cloth dampened with neutral cleaner to remove dirt and water stains.
3. Water outage or insufficient water pressure: Wait for water supply to resume. If water pressure remains persistently low, install a booster pump to increase pressure (applicable water pressure range: 0.05–0.7 MPa).
4. Filter screen clogged with debris: Remove the external filter screen, rinse with clean water, and reinstall.
5. Power outage (AC 220V model): Wait for power to resume, or switch to battery power mode (if supported).

---

### Q: The sensor faucet won't stop running water – what could be the issue? | tap,trouble

A: Possible causes and solutions:
1. Battery depleted: Replace with new AA alkaline batteries.
2. Water pressure outside the applicable range: Check whether water pressure is between 0.05–0.7 MPa. If too low, install a booster pump; if too high, install a pressure reducing valve.
3. Sensor window obstructed by foreign objects / false triggering: Clear any foreign objects near the sensor window and ensure there are no persistent obstructions within the sensing range.
4. Impurities clogging the solenoid valve diaphragm: Disassemble the solenoid valve assembly, rinse with clean water, and reinstall.

---

### Q: Can an electric heating faucet be fitted with a sensor spout? | tap

A: There are two types of electric heating faucets on the market: one with an overheating protection switch, which can be fitted; the other without overheating protection, which cannot be fitted, as installation may damage the electric heating faucet. However, it is difficult for users to distinguish whether overheating protection is present, so it is recommended to directly purchase the company's pre-assembled complete electric heating faucet + sensor spout kit (available from the company).
Troubleshooting – Cannot be installed:
Verify applicable faucet height: Suitable for high-arc faucets where the original faucet spout height above the countertop is 25 cm (measure the spout height first). Low-arc or other irregular faucets below this height, once fitted with the Smart GIBO Xiaoxibao sensor spout, will be too close to the countertop, easily causing false sensing and making use impossible or degrading the user experience. Installation is not recommended.
Verify applicable faucet spout: Compatible with faucets having internal thread 24 mm and external thread 22 mm, fitting approximately 80% of faucets on the market. Remove the aerator (spout tip) from the faucet and use a ruler or a 0.5 RMB coin to measure the diameter and internal/external thread type of the faucet or aerator threaded end. If matching, installation is possible.
Note: Our company can customize adapter rings of several other diameters, shipped as needed. Please confirm with customer service before shipping.
To measure the thread diameter of your household spout, refer to the diagram below:

### Q: How to determine water pressure? | tap,toilet

A: Water pressure confirmation:
1. Tap water supply requires 1.5–3.5 kg, but end-point supply is generally 1.5 kg.
2. Floors 1–10 belong to the low-pressure supply zone, while floors 11–18 belong to the high-pressure supply zone. Floors 11–16 have sufficient water pressure; floors 17–20 require confirmation; floors 20–26 also require checking.
3. Floors 1–6 are raw water supply; floors 6–10 are secondary water supply where pressure may be low and requires confirmation. The same applies to higher floors.
4. If kitchen faucet output in 30 seconds is less than 4.5 liters, water pressure is low; if greater than 7 liters, pressure is high. (Customers are advised to use the water-discharge method to measure floor water pressure themselves.)
5. Consult the property management for the building's water supply pressure.
End-point tap water supply is generally 1.5–3.5 kg.
The tankless K11 Smart Toilet requires water pressure greater than 1.5 kg.
Because high-rise residential buildings require secondary water supply, with booster pumps every 10 floors, water pressure must be measured on floors 6–10, 16–20, and 26–30.

---

### Q: What materials are commonly used for valve bodies? What are their advantages? | tap

A: PPS, PPO, PPA, PA9T, PA66+GF, etc. (nylon-modified engineering plastics and specialty engineering plastics).
Advantages:
Low water absorption; dimensional stability is not affected by water absorption, preventing dimensional changes and mechanical strength degradation.
High mechanical strength, good toughness.
High heat resistance, low temperature resistance.
Higher cost.

### Q: What materials are commonly used for faucet bodies? What are the differences? | tap

A: Common faucet body materials:
Brass alloy (e.g., Brass 59-1): High-end appearance, good formability, high shape plasticity, diverse electroplating and PVD surface treatment options, heavy weight, higher cost.
Zinc alloy die-casting: Similar appearance and texture to brass; compared to brass, lighter weight and relatively lower cost.
Brass or stainless steel round/square tubes: Typically used for spout-type sensor faucets.
New composite materials: Metal exterior + glass, PMMA, etc., combined processes to highlight smart features such as temperature display and illuminated ring indicators.

### Q: What is the principle behind anti-false-trigger sensor faucets? How effective is GIBO's anti-false-trigger performance? | tap

A: Anti-false-trigger sensor faucets employ infrared dual-sensing + AI algorithms, effectively filtering out interference from light, water vapor, and reflections. They only respond to human presence, preventing unintended water flow. GIBO's independently developed anti-false-trigger algorithm, built on 20 years of technical expertise, delivers stable anti-false-trigger performance across various complex lighting and humid environments, preventing water waste.

---

### Q: What does standby current ≤ 100 μA mean for a sensor faucet? How much electricity can be saved in actual use? | tap

A: Standby current ≤ 100 μA qualifies as an industry ultra-low-power standard for sensor faucets. In simple terms, 4 AA batteries can last from several months to 1 year. Compared to ordinary sensor faucets, this reduces battery replacement frequency significantly each year, greatly lowering maintenance costs, especially suitable for high-frequency commercial use scenarios.

---

### Q: Are sensor faucets affected by lighting or sunlight interference? How do GIBO products avoid interference? | tap

A: Ordinary sensor faucets are susceptible to interference from lighting, sunlight, and reflections, causing false triggers. GIBO sensor faucets are equipped with proprietary anti-light-interference algorithms that effectively filter out interference from common lighting, sunlight, and reflections, responding only to human presence. This prevents unintended water flow and adapts to various complex lighting environments.

---

### Q: Does the sensor window of a sensor faucet get dirty easily? Does dirt affect sensing performance? | tap,sensor

A: The sensor window is made of anti-fouling material and is not prone to dirt accumulation. Routine daily cleaning with a dry cloth is sufficient. If the sensor window becomes stained with dirt or scale, it may affect sensing sensitivity. Simply wiping it clean with warm water will restore normal sensing performance. Maintenance is simple and convenient.

---

### Q: What does standby current ≤ 30 μA mean for a sensor faucet? How much electricity can be saved in actual use? | tap

A: μA stands for microampere, where 1 μA equals one-millionth of an ampere. ≤ 30 μA means that in standby mode with no one using the faucet, the circuit consumes extremely minimal current. Taking 4 AA batteries (total capacity approximately 8000 mAh) as an example, the theoretical standby time exceeds 30,000 hours (approximately 3.4 years). In actual public settings with frequent use, battery life typically still reaches 1–2 years, substantially saving on battery replacement costs and manual maintenance costs.

---

### Q: What waterproof rating is suitable for sensor faucets? What should be noted when installing in humid environments? | tap

A: The waterproof rating of a sensor faucet should be selected based on the usage environment. Commercial scenarios require at least IPX4 waterproofing; humid environments such as bathrooms and shower rooms are recommended to use IPX5 waterproof rating. All GIBO engineering-grade sensor faucets meet IPX4 or higher waterproof ratings, requiring no additional protection when installed in humid environments, adapting to various humid conditions.

---

### Q: What is the difference between a sensor hand-wash machine and a soap dispenser faucet? Which venues are they respectively suitable for? | tap,soap

A: The core difference lies in functional integration. A sensor hand-wash machine independently dispenses foam/liquid, suitable for scenarios requiring standalone hand cleaning, such as hospitals, schools, and shopping mall restrooms. A soap dispenser faucet integrates water and soap dispensing in one unit, saving more space, suitable for hotel bathrooms, residential bathrooms, and small commercial venues. GIBO has mature products in both categories and supports selection based on needs.

---

### Q: Is residential sensor faucet installation complicated? Does it require pre-wired power? | tap

### Q: What is the difference between recessed cisterns and plastic cisterns? | flusher

A: Plastic cisterns age and crack over time, making water stains and scale difficult to clean. Plastic cisterns all require booster pumps for water supply, producing loud noise and having a short service life.
Recessed cisterns have ceramic thickness up to 7 mm, will not age or crack even after 20–30 years, and feature multi-layer glazed internal piping that resists staining. Even when dirty, a simple wipe with a cloth cleans them. The integrated one-piece piping uses large-diameter automatic water fittings for flushing, with no noise and better results.

---

### Q: Why does a recessed cistern cost RMB 400 more than a pulse flush? | flusher,toilet

A: Toilets with recessed cisterns involve complex technology and manufacturing processes, making them difficult to mold. That's why only branded products priced above RMB 5,000 feature recessed cisterns.
Pulse flushing does not require a cistern. Recessed cistern flushing, with automatic water inlet and flush fittings, provides greater flushing power without water pressure concerns.

---

### Q: What is siphon jet powerful flushing? | flusher,toilet

A: The siphon principle is based on the communicating vessel principle. An inverted S-shaped siphon tube is installed in the toilet, operating using atmospheric pressure. After filling the siphon tube with water, the S-tube contains no air, and the higher water pressure at the inlet end pushes water and waste continuously out of the outlet. During flushing, when the water level inside the siphon toilet exceeds the high point of the S-bend, a siphon effect is created, drawing water and waste out together. When only a small amount of water remains, the siphon effect is broken, leaving a small amount of water that forms the toilet's water seal, providing odor prevention.
Our recessed cistern's original super-cyclone siphon jet flushing technology uses pressurized jet water flow along the inner wall of the toilet bowl, creating a vortex-type wash that removes stains from the inner wall. Simultaneously, inertia amplifies the siphon flushing force, using powerful vortex and 360° turbine action to increase the toilet's flushing coverage area, forming all-directional powerful water flow scouring, supplemented by a bottom jet water column boost to achieve siphon jet powerful flushing – removing all waste in a single flush.

---

### Q: Are the remote controls for sensor modules universal? What functions do they have? | sensor

A: The remote controls used by GIBO sensor modules are divided into function remotes and mode remotes, both of which can be used with all infrared sensor modules except laser sensor modules.
Function remotes are primarily used to adjust sensing distance and flush duration, intended for end-user use.

### Q: What are the functional components of microwave sensing? | sensor

A: Microwave sensing, commonly known as radar sensing, differs from infrared sensor modules in that microwave radar sensor modules do not require an infrared sensing window, thus maintaining the complete appearance of the unit.
Radar is an electronic device that detects targets using electromagnetic waves. It emits electromagnetic waves to illuminate the target and receives the reflected waves, thereby obtaining information such as the distance from the target to the emission point, rate of distance change (radial velocity), azimuth, and angle.
The microwave module uses imported ST chips for control, with low-power design, remote-adjustable sensing distance of 0–100 cm, 256 ms response speed achieving instant lid opening upon approach, high waterproof performance, and more stable operation.

---

### Q: Is dTOF Laser sensing power consumption higher than infrared? | sensor

A: Under normal circumstances, laser sensing power consumption is higher than traditional infrared. The industry average generally requires about 200 μA. After 2 years of research and optimization, GIBO has reduced laser sensing power consumption to below 60 μA, meeting national standard requirements.

---

### Q: Why is dTOF Laser sensing so expensive? | sensor

A: Laser sensing is primarily applied in high-precision measurement, automotive electronics, and similar application fields, demanding high technical requirements and complex processes. There are relatively few selectable laser module suppliers, and component prices are significantly higher than infrared.

---

### Q: What is good about laser sensing? | sensor

A: dTOF Laser sensing exhibits very small distance variation across different colors and is not affected by mirror-surface material reflections. There is no self-triggering issue. Because laser uses a unidirectional light source, anti-interference capability is greatly enhanced, and overall performance is significantly improved compared to infrared.

---

### Q: Which products is laser sensing suitable for? | sensor

A: dTOF Laser sensing is suitable for products requiring precise distance control, such as faucet spout sensing, squat pan sensors, and toilet sensors.

---

### Q: Is laser the same as infrared? What is ToF? | sensor

A: Strictly speaking, apart from ultraviolet and visible light, all other light belongs to infrared. Laser refers to photons generated by stimulated emission of radiation – it is the process of strengthening or weakening a certain light (e.g., infrared, red, blue, etc.) through a laser generator for emission. What we currently apply can be considered infrared light.
ToF: Time of Flight. ToF is essentially a ranging method that calculates the distance between two points by measuring the "flight time" of signals such as ultrasound, microwave, or light between the emitter and reflector.
Data generated and captured by ToF sensors is highly useful because it provides depth information. Combined with cameras, it yields 3D images, enabling pedestrian detection, user identity authentication based on facial features, environmental mapping using SLAM (Simultaneous Localization and Mapping) algorithms, and more. ToF sensors are widely applied in mobile phones, robotics, smart vehicles, AR, and various other fields.

---

### Q: How does laser sensing handle black-and-white objects without being affected by reflections? | sensor

A: First, laser sensing uses the ToF time-of-flight ranging method, offering high ranging accuracy. Second, the laser beam is monochromatic, containing only a single specific wavelength of light, and therefore is not affected by object color. Third, special optical filter technology is used to reduce reflection and interference.

---

### Q: Can traditional infrared sensing be directly upgraded to laser sensing? | sensor

A: Laser sensing and infrared sensing circuit boards cannot be directly upgraded. The laser solution requires separate software, hardware, and structural upgrades and optimization. Laser has specific requirements for the material and color of the sensor window, requiring new custom mold development for a laser-specific sensor window. Additionally, separate matching design of the hardware circuit is needed.

---

### Q: Does laser sensing have size/volume requirements? | sensor

A: With the miniaturization of third-generation laser sensor modules, the volume limitation issue in the sensor sanitary ware field has been largely resolved. Most existing sensor window dimensions can meet usage requirements.

---

### Q: Are there intellectual property issues with laser sensing? | sensor

A: Laser is a general-purpose technology with no intellectual property issues. However, laser technology applications on different products may involve patent protection. It is recommended to carry out IP protection work during the new product R&D phase. GIBO's sensor sanitary ware control module using laser sensing has already applied for related patents in 2019 and can be licensed for use: "A Sensor Circuit for Sensor Sanitary Ware ZL201922103799.8."

---

### Q: What is the difference between laser sensing and infrared sensing? | sensor

A: Compared to infrared sensing, laser has several major advantages: more precise sensing distance, insensitive to color, effective prevention of reflections from glass, ceramic, and mirror surfaces avoiding self-triggering, and better anti-interference performance.
However, laser sensing has higher power consumption compared to infrared sensing.

---

Connectors use waterproof designs, achieving an IP65 waterproof rating.

### Q: Can the sensor window still be used if it is scratched? | sensor,trouble

A: Scratches on the sensor window will severely affect sensing distance and sensing sensitivity, although in most cases functionality can still operate normally.
All GIBO sensor windows are made of high-strength scratch-resistant PC material, capable of withstanding long-term use without easily developing scratches. For maintenance, cleaning with a soft cloth is recommended. Do not use corrosive liquids or steel wool for cleaning.

### Q: Why does the sensor window need to be mold-produced by GIBO? | sensor,odm

A: The sensor window is a core raw material of the sensor module. The material, scratch resistance, and light transmittance of the sensor window all affect sensor performance.
Most sensor windows use branded polycarbonate (PC) material, which has high strength and high-temperature resistance, low shrinkage, effective resistance to thermal expansion and contraction, improving waterproof performance and service life.
The surface material has scratch resistance with hardness reaching H grade, preventing scratches during maintenance and use that would affect sensing distance.
A specially customized formulation provides good light transmittance and superior performance, not replaceable by ordinary materials currently on the market.
The structure and process must be adapted to the circuit board and light-isolating sleeve to ensure low power consumption and waterproof performance.

### Q: Why does the sensor module need triple waterproofing? | sensor

A: Sensors are generally used in restrooms. Especially for sensor sanitary ware used in public settings, the usage environment is harsh, requiring moisture-proof and anti-corrosion measures.
Corrosive gases significantly impact the service life of sensor sanitary ware, causing accelerated oxidation of electronic components and circuit board corrosion, leading to various operational malfunctions.
Humid air can penetrate circuit boards, causing short circuits, open circuits, and other tracking failures. Simultaneously, moisture can form condensation mist inside the sensor window, causing shortened distance, self-triggering, or inability to sense.
The triple waterproofing process not only applies conformal coating on the PCB but also incorporates internal cavity structure design in the sensor window to prevent thermal expansion and contraction effects, along with epoxy resin potting. This achieves an overall IP67 waterproof rating, ensuring that even in harsh public restroom environments, product service life is not shortened, delivering stable and reliable performance.

---

### Q: What are the key factors affecting sensor module quality? | sensor

A: Sensor circuit quality is closely related to design experience. Based on 18 years of industry experience and over 180 technology patents, GIBO delivers a service life of up to 500,000 cycles, providing products and solutions for mid-to-high-end clients in the industry.
Key factors include:
Matching selection of industrial-grade electronic components;
Anti-interference design of PCB and hardware;
Low-power algorithms and anti-interference algorithms in software;
Precision waterproof structural design and waterproofing processes.

---

### Q: How long can four AA batteries last? | sensor

A: We use low-power electronic components and hold proprietary micro-power energy-saving patents. The product standby power consumption is less than 30 μA. Based on 200 uses per day in typical public settings, even with 4 AA batteries, operation can last up to 18 months.
In GIBO's laboratory environment using continuous testing methods, 4 AA batteries can support over 200,000 cycles of use.

---

### Q: What service life can the sensor module achieve? | sensor

A: A sensor module is an electronic product. Generally speaking, the production of electronic components follows strict quality control systems with low defect rates and high precision. Commercial-grade products can be used at 0–70°C; industrial-grade at -40–85°C; automotive-grade at -40–125°C; military-grade at -55–150°C.
GIBO sensor modules all use industrial-grade electronic components, offering wide applicability and long durability. The national standard is 200,000 cycles. GIBO's standard factory specification is 500,000-cycle service life, and the general design service life exceeds 1 million cycles.

---

### Q: What are the main manufacturing processes of sensor modules? | sensor

A: The main manufacturing processes of sensor modules include: PCB Layout, PCB etching/corrosion, PCB depaneling, SMT component placement processing, AOI inspection, discrete component wire soldering, control program burning, functional and performance testing, epoxy resin potting, finished product testing.
Before leaving the factory, sensor modules undergo AOI inspection, distance and function debugging, 3 rounds of full performance inspection, and OQC inspection, ensuring 100% quality compliance.

---

### Q: Why does GIBO's sensor module offer superior performance? | sensor

A: Material selection: Industrial-grade branded electronic components are used.
Process: High manufacturing process requirements – anti-static workshop, advanced SMT processes.
Technology: 20 years of accumulated industry experience, a complex anti-interference algorithm software library, and a deeper understanding of customer needs.

---

### Q: What are the minimum dimensions of the sensor circuit board? | sensor

A: Integrated: 14.8 mm × 23.9 mm
Split: 7 mm × 17.5 mm
Round split: 10 mm × 12.9 mm
Round integrated: Uses the same board as the standard integrated type – 14.8 mm × 23.9 mm

---

### Q: What is the difference between 3V, 4.5V, and 6V power supply? | sensor

A: The operating voltage of MCUs and electronic components is generally feasible from 2.2V to 5.5V. Currently, output loads applied in smart bathroom applications are generally solenoid valves and motors, with operating voltages typically in the range of 3.0–6V. Therefore, whether AC or DC power supply, the voltage supply requirements can be met.
However, battery-powered products have higher requirements for power consumption and battery life. Different control schemes have certain differences in fully utilizing the battery. Different schemes control different loads. Solenoid valves on the market may be DC 4.5V or 3V, with differing drive current and operating voltage requirements. Control principles also vary depending on the load – 3V and 4.5V require boost schemes, while 6V can drive directly.

---

### Q: Do all sensor modules have power-off protection? | sensor

A: According to the CJ/T 194-2014 "Non-contact Water Supply Fixtures" industry standard, every product must have low-voltage warning and power-off protection functions. When powered by battery or power adapter, under any circumstances, the solenoid valve must be in the closed state within 1 second after power loss.
Currently, many products on the market lack power-off protection functionality.

---

### Q: What is the difference between power-off protection and low-voltage warning? | sensor

A: Low-voltage warning function: When the battery voltage drops below 4.5V, the solenoid valve closes and stops operating. The LED indicator flashes 10 times at 1-second intervals. (Test voltage 4.5 ± 0.1V)
Power-off protection function: When powered by battery or power adapter, under any circumstances, the solenoid valve must be in the closed state within 1 second after power loss.
The low-voltage warning primarily serves to prompt battery replacement, preventing erroneous operation when battery power is insufficient. Power-off protection ensures the solenoid valve can close under any circumstances.

---

### Q: What type of output signal does the sensor module produce? | sensor

A: Generally, the output signal is a pulse signal or level signal. A level signal is represented by voltage level values, divided into high level "1" and low level "0". A pulse signal is emitted continuously at a certain voltage amplitude and time interval. The time interval between pulse signals is called the period; the number of pulses generated per unit time (e.g., 1 second) is called frequency.
Pulse solenoid valves generally open with a positive pulse and close with a negative pulse.

---

### Q: Is adaptive sensing distance practical? | sensor

A: Sensor sanitary ware products, whether used in homes or public venues, are multi-user products that need to accommodate the usage habits and methods of most people. Under normal circumstances, automatic distance adjustment or adaptive distance adjustment is not recommended. Products prone to self-triggering issues due to distance problems are generally spout sensor products. Adaptive distance adjustment cannot resolve all usage scenario issues, such as basin reflection, light reflection, and drain mirror reflection.
It is recommended to use laser sensing technology, which offers stable distance, higher precision, and stronger anti-interference capability, to solve the self-triggering problems of spout sensing and the distance-shortening problems of flush valves in actual use.

---

### Q: Does the sensor module have reverse-polarity protection? | sensor

A: Reverse-polarity protection means that when the product's power connection wires and solenoid valve connection wires are in a reversed positive/negative polarity state, no adverse phenomena such as product heating or component breakdown should occur. After reverse-polarity testing, the product should operate normally when connected with the correct polarity.
All GIBO sensor module power inputs have reverse-polarity protection, so there is no need to worry about burnout damage caused by reversed connections.

---

### Q: What is self-triggering? Why does it occur? | sensor

A: Self-triggering is when the sensor enters a continuous operating state due to light reflection caused by obstacles or interference sources in front of the sensor. Common manifestations include: ceramic basin reflection self-triggering, spout water column reflection self-triggering, stainless steel and other mirror-surface reflection self-triggering, and strong sunlight reflection self-triggering.

---

### Q: Does a continuously flashing indicator light mean the battery is dead? | sensor

A: A flashing red indicator light on the sensor module indicates that the monitored supply voltage is below 4.6V. The sensor has entered low-voltage warning mode, requiring timely battery replacement to ensure normal product operation.

---

### Q: How many indicator light states does the sensor module have? What are they? | sensor

A: Indicator light states serve to better identify the product's operating status. Different products may have slightly different indication states. The main states are as follows:
Standby state: DC products generally default to no indicator; AC products can be set to indicator always on.
Operating state: When entering the sensing zone, the indicator is always on; when leaving the sensing zone, the indicator turns off.
Low-voltage warning: When entering the sensing zone, the indicator flashes; when leaving the sensing zone, the indicator turns off.
No-power state: When entering the sensing zone, the indicator flashes rapidly and continuously; when leaving the sensing zone, the indicator turns off.
Abnormal state: Yellow light flashes.
Indicator colors: Commonly used are red, yellow, green, and blue.

---

### Q: Why does the sensor still activate after my hand leaves the sensing range? What is drag distance? | sensor

A: Drag distance can be understood as: the hand normally enters the sensing range, the sensor activates. During sensor operation, as the hand slowly moves away from the sensor in the opposite direction, when it exceeds the stated sensing distance range, the sensor is still operating. Continuing the reverse motion until the sensor stops, the distance measured beyond the stated sensing distance to the point where sensing stops is considered the drag distance.
Generally, to provide a better user experience, sensors set a certain drag distance to prevent frequent water on/off switching at the critical distance boundary, which would affect the user experience.
Typically, the drag distance is controlled to no more than 20% of the stated sensing distance.

---

### Q: Why is there a deviation in the sensing distance reported by customers? | sensor

A: Under normal circumstances, every product undergoes strict functional and performance testing before leaving the factory, with sensing distance calibrated according to factory requirements. If there is a deviation in sensing distance measured in the customer's laboratory, the test environment should be calibrated or excluded, including ambient light brightness, standard whiteboard, test methods, and interference sources.
A frequently occurring deviation is that customers use human hand sensing to measure distance during testing, whereas the factory standard is testing with a standard whiteboard. The distance measured by human hand testing versus standard whiteboard testing may deviate by approximately 30–40%.

---

### Q: What is the standard for sensing distance testing? | sensor

A: Generally, a standard whiteboard is used as the standard for distance testing. The standard whiteboard specification is 30 × 30 cm, using 80 g A4 paper as the surface paper with double-sided adhesion. Sensing must not be triggered outside the required distance range, and must be triggered within the required distance range. Sensing distance error within 10% is considered acceptable.
During distance testing, multiple materials and colors are tested using a Kodak 18° gray card, whiteboard, and blackboard. Testing different reflective surfaces (such as mirrors, bright stainless steel plates, whiteboards, blackboards, PU, and other materials), the sensing distance deviation between comparison values must not exceed 15%.

---

### Q: What is the relationship between sensor module output signal and load? | sensor

A: Sensor module output signals are generally pulse signals and level signals, with the appropriate output signal selected based on the specific load.
Pilot-operated pulse solenoid valves use pulse signal control with a pulse width of 15 ms–40 ms, suitable for low-power applications.
Direct-acting solenoid valves generally use level signal control, controlled by output open (1) and close (0) signals.
DC motors are generally controlled through PWM (Pulse Width Modulation) to manage forward/reverse rotation and speed.

---

### Q: What are the testing standards for sensor modules? | sensor

A: Generally, testing is based on CJ/T 194-2014 "Non-contact Water Supply Fixtures," national standard GB 14536.1 "Automatic Electrical Controls for Household and Similar Use," EU EN 60335-1 "Electrical Safety Testing," EU EN 61000 "Electromagnetic Radiation Testing (EMC)," and relevant enterprise standards.

---

### Q: How is GIBO's anti-interference performance better than competitors? | sensor

A: Generally, the primary anti-interference concerns for infrared sensors are light source interference and EMC interference. GIBO sensor anti-interference performance reaches Level 4A standard, with performance indicators significantly exceeding the industry average (Level 2A).
GIBO's laboratory can conduct all interference tests except EMC: 23 types of common light source interference (national standard: 3 types), 15KV anti-static discharge interference (national standard: 6KV), 5KV (5 kHz) burst pulse anti-interference (national standard: 4KV), and multiple common electrical appliance interference tests.
At the same time, GIBO sensor module products, after 18 years of technical accumulation, have undergone deep software anti-interference optimization, ensuring that products used in various public settings will not experience random water flow or non-sensing phenomena. The anti-interference algorithms are protected by national patents and software copyrights.

---

### Q: What certifications can sensor modules pass? | sensor

A: Products can pass EU CE certification, RoHS certification, North American FCC certification, and EMC certification, suitable for sale in global markets.

---

A: Most products and projects have existing ready-made solutions. For simple parameter modifications such as sensing distance and flush duration adjustments, and simple function additions, in principle, no separate software development fee is charged to the customer. However, hardware PCB prototyping costs of RMB 600 must be paid separately.

The above development fees are only apportioned based on developer salaries. Specific projects are assessed and billed based on actual project evaluation. The above serves only as a reference for conventional project fees.

---

### Q: Is the sensor window made of ordinary plastic? What are its advantages? | sensor

A: All GIBO sensor windows use PC (polycarbonate). Advantages: excellent optical properties. PC high-molecular-weight resin has very high toughness and impact strength. PC material has flame retardancy and oxidation resistance.

---

### Q: The infrared sensor remote control is not responsive – what to do? | sensor,trouble

A: Troubleshoot from 3 points: 1. Check remote control battery level (when voltage < 2.7 ± 0.2V, sensing may be unresponsive; replace the battery). 2. Clear obstructions in the sensing area (such as water stains, dust) and keep the sensing area clean. 3. Ensure the sensing distance is within the effective range (≤ 8 m) without significant obstructions, and align the sensing angle with the receiving end.

---

### Q: Does the product have components that are easy to replace and repair? | sensor

A: Products generally adopt a modular design philosophy. Most products and accessories are designed based on easy replacement and repairability. Core components including sensor modules, circuit assemblies, solenoid valves, and power supplies all support quick replacement.
At the same time, after product discontinuation, a certain quantity of repair spare parts is generally reserved to meet repair needs for 3–5 years post-discontinuation.

---

### Q: What is the MOQ for sensor modules? | sensor,odm

A: The production process uses online programming/burning technology, which can meet the small-batch customization needs of more clients as much as possible. However, customization must consider sensor window compatibility and software function adjustments.
Without sensor window compatibility: new sensor window mold required, MOQ = 2,000 pcs.
With sensor window compatibility, PCB change required: re-customize PCB, MOQ = 1,000 pcs.
With sensor window compatibility, minor software adjustment: no PCB customization needed, only software parameters and code modification, MOQ = 300 pcs.

### Q: How to select Smart Showers for bulk procurement by large senior care centers? | shower

### Q: Can the thermostatic function be locked? To prevent children or elderly from accidentally adjusting the temperature? | shower

A: Yes, constant temperature lock function is supported. Long-press the "Temperature Adjustment" key on the touch panel for 3 seconds to lock the currently set temperature. Once locked, neither the wireless remote nor the panel can adjust water temperature. Long-press again for 3 seconds to unlock, balancing convenience and safety.

---

### Q: Is the thermostatic function effective? Will the temperature fluctuate between hot and cold? | shower

A: The thermostatic performance of this product is stable. Equipped with the HW05 intelligent thermostatic valve cartridge, temperature control precision is plus or minus 2 degrees C. With cold water at 18-20 degrees C, hot water at 65-70 degrees C, and water pressure at 0.3MPa, thermostatic response time is under 3 seconds. When cold/hot water temperature or water pressure fluctuates, the system automatically and quickly adjusts to avoid sudden temperature changes. It also features over-temperature protection (above or equal to 49 degrees C, automatic water shutoff), eliminating scalding risk.

---

### Q: Does the pre-drainage function consume a lot of electricity? | shower

A: No, the instantaneous power consumption of the pre-drainage function is extremely low. Moreover, this product uses a dual power supply mode of hydroelectric generator plus lithium battery. During pre-drainage, the hydroelectric generator simultaneously charges the lithium battery. In daily use, there is no need to worry about the pre-drainage function consuming excessive power -- it is energy-efficient and durable.

---

### Q: After cold or hot water supply is lost, can the thermostatic function still operate normally? | shower

A: After cold or hot water supply is lost, the thermostatic function automatically enters protection mode and no longer regulates water temperature. When cold water supply is lost within 5 seconds with output water volume under or equal to 200mL, water temperature remains under or equal to 49 degrees C. When hot water supply is lost within 5 seconds with output water volume under or equal to 250mL, this avoids dry running that could damage components. After water supply is restored, the thermostatic function automatically resets with water temperature deviation under or equal to 2K, quickly returning to normal thermostatic status.

---

### Q: Does the Shower have a nighttime sleep function? | shower

A: GIBO Smart Showers can be customized with a nighttime sleep mode. During set time periods (e.g., 22:00-06:00), the device automatically enters low-power standby. The digital display screen turns off and the sensor circuit shuts down, reducing Standby Power to under or equal to 0.1mW. On first use the next day, pressing any key triggers quick wake-up and returns to normal operation within 3 seconds. This function is particularly suitable for hotel rooms, apartments, and other venues unused at night. One device can save approximately 3-5 kWh of standby electricity per year. With multi-unit scaled usage, the energy-saving effect is even more significant.

### Q: How to perform daily maintenance to extend service life? | shower

A: Simply follow these 3 points: (1) Regularly wipe the digital display panel and remote control to prevent long-term water stain residue. (2) Avoid sharp objects impacting the device to prevent component damage. (3) When not in use for extended periods, turn off the power, fully charge the lithium battery before storage, and top up the charge every 3 months to avoid damage from deep discharge.

### Q: Will water splash randomly during pre-drainage? Can the pre-drained cold water be recycled? | shower

A: No, water will not splash randomly. During pre-drainage, the system automatically opens the "bottom outlet" Solenoid Valve, and cold water flows out from the bottom outlet nozzle. It can be directly connected to a drain or collected in a container for recycling (such as toilet flushing or mopping), saving energy and avoiding waste.

---

### Q: Can water temperature be manually adjusted? What is the adjustment range? | shower

A: Yes, dual adjustment via wireless remote control and panel touch is supported. Water temperature setting range is 20.0 degrees C to 48.0 degrees C. ### Q: Can the Cold Water Pre-drainage duration be set? What is the maximum pre-drainage time? | shower

A: Manual setting of pre-drainage duration is not currently supported. The system default maximum pre-drainage duration is 180 seconds (3 minutes). Once this duration is reached, pre-drainage stops automatically. If the water temperature reaches 38 plus or minus 2 degrees C early (sustained for 2 seconds), it will also stop early, ensuring no cold water waiting while avoiding water resource waste.

---

### Q: Can the digital display screen brightness be adjusted? Is it too glaring for nighttime use? | shower

A: The digital display screen of this product features fixed brightness design. The brightness is soft and non-glaring, suitable for nighttime shower scenarios with no need for manual adjustment. The digital display screen only illuminates when discharging water, during pre-drainage, or showing fault alerts. Once water is shut off, it automatically turns off, not affecting nighttime rest.

Excessive obstructions will affect the signal; operation in areas without significant obstructions is recommended.

### Q: Can one wireless remote control operate multiple Shower devices? | shower

A: No, one wireless remote control can only pair with one receiving unit (shower control assembly), avoiding simultaneous response from multiple devices that would cause operating confusion. ### Q: Can the panel touch controls be locked? To prevent accidental shutdown or temperature change while showering? | shower

A: Yes, panel touch control locking is supported. Press and hold the "Power On/Off" key on the touch panel for 5 seconds to lock all touch functions. After locking, touch controls are unresponsive. Long-press the "Power On/Off" key again for 5 seconds to unlock, effectively preventing accidental triggering from water splashes during showering and ensuring a good showering experience.

### Q: How is this product powered? How long does the lithium battery last? Is frequent charging needed? | shower

A: It uses a dual power supply mode: "hydroelectric generator + 3.7V lithium polymer battery + USB Type-C backup charging." During daily use, the hydroelectric generator automatically starts when water flows, charging the lithium battery for energy storage. Standby Power is under or equal to 0.1mW, energy-efficient and durable. For normal home/hotel use, no frequent charging is needed. When the lithium battery is depleted, it can be manually recharged via the USB Type-C interface.

### Q: The Cold Water Pre-drainage has no effect; I still have to wait a long time for hot water. What is the reason? | shower,trouble

A: There are mainly 3 situations to check one by one: (1) Pre-drainage was not successfully triggered (it must be triggered by one-touch via wireless remote or panel touch; upon triggering, the buzzer sounds "beep" once). (2) There is too much cold water in the piping and the pre-drainage stop condition has not been met (water temperature reaching over or equal to 38 plus or minus 2 degrees C sustained for 2 seconds, or pre-drainage exceeding 180 seconds -- simply wait patiently). (3) NTC sensor fault (the digital display will show a fault indicator, and the buzzer will alarm. Contact after-sales to replace the sensor).

### Q: How to turn off/switch the automatic lid opening function? | toilet

To re-enable automatic lid opening, repeat the above operation. Two beeps from the main unit indicate that the automatic lid opening function is enabled.

### Q: What are the functional components of the automatic lid opening feature? | toilet

POM self-lubricating components achieve silent lid opening. Precision gear design gives the product a service life exceeding 200,000 cycles. The toilet lid uses a soft-close damper with precision mold design. The internal damper gears are filled with specialized silicone oil, allowing the product to contact damping oil and rotate, generating torque for longer gear service life while achieving silent and smooth lid opening and closing.

### Q: What is the difference between instant-heat and tank-heat types? | toilet

A: Instant-heat type provides 1-second instant heating within the water pipe without repeatedly heating water stored in a tank. It is healthy and energy-saving with zero stored water and unlimited capacity, preventing harmful bacteria from breeding during long-term water storage. Tank-heat type repeatedly circulates and heats the water in the tank, which over time is prone to scale buildup and bacteria growth, posing significant harm to intimate areas.

# Chapter 8 ODM Customization (Tag: odm, 64 entries total)

### Q: How much do patent registration and application fees cost? | odm

A: Patent applications generally involve official application fees, registration fees, examination fees, agency fees, and annual fees. Design patents and utility model patents generally have an official fee of RMB 500 and agency fee of RMB 1,500 for a total of RMB 2,000. Annual fees of RMB 600-2,000 begin from the second year, increasing annually. Invention patents generally have an official fee of RMB 950, agency fee of RMB 3,000-5,000, and examination fee of RMB 3,450. Annual fees of RMB 900-8,000 begin from the second year, increasing annually. PCT international patents can cover over 150 countries and regions worldwide with a single application valid globally. Total costs generally include application fees, search fees, and examination fees, costing approximately USD 5,000-8,000.

---

### Q: Can 3D-printed prototype samples be used for functional testing? | odm

A: 3D-printed prototype samples can be used for functional testing (but not for fatigue and lifespan testing). Through 3D printing technology, physical models can be created based on design files. These models can effectively demonstrate the product's appearance and structure and can simulate the functional characteristics of the actual product. Through functional testing, the product's performance under different operating conditions can be evaluated, potential issues can be identified, and improvements and optimizations can be made. Therefore, 3D-printed prototype samples play a very important role in the product development process, providing reference and feedback to help the design team continuously refine the product's functionality and performance.

After mold fabrication, formal samples are provided for functional verification to ensure functionality and performance meet design requirements. After small-batch trial production, formal production products are provided for functional verification and sample sealing.

### Q: Is mold ownership shared or exclusive? | odm

A: Mold ownership supports both shared and exclusive cooperation models, as follows: If the customer requires market exclusivity for the final product, all mold-related costs are borne by the customer. If the customer does not require market exclusivity for the final product, the cost-sharing model is supported: each party bears 50% of the mold cost to reduce development costs and maximize the utilization of development results. Because GIBO only charges cost price for molds, with design and maintenance costs borne by GIBO, for maximum economic benefit: if within 2 years the customer fails to meet the order volume committed in the cooperation agreement, regardless of whether mold ownership is shared or exclusive, GIBO will automatically possess the relevant disposal rights for that mold, including mold modification, scrapping, and ownership transfer. However, ownership, cost, and profit-sharing arrangements must be based on friendly negotiation and agreement with the partner to be valid.

### Q: How is the project development schedule and project cycle determined? | odm

A: Project development is primarily divided into four stages: requirements initiation, development design, mold fabrication, and formal production. Requirements initiation generally takes 5-10 days to complete project requirements review and confirm cooperation intent between both parties. Development design generally takes 30-45 days, including hardware/software design, structural design, design review, 3D sample confirmation, and design improvement. Mold fabrication generally takes 35-45 days, including drawing output, mold design, mold base procurement, mold machining, trial mold verification, and formal sample confirmation. Formal production generally takes 35-60 days, including document output, workflow development, trial production, material procurement, formal production, and shipment. Generally, assuming no mid-project requirement changes and no technical bottlenecks, based on project difficulty, progress, and abnormal situations, there may be a 10-20 day deviation from the estimated cycle.

### Q: How much do product certification fees cost? | odm

A: Product certification fees vary significantly depending on different countries and regions and different certification bodies. Specific inquiries should be directed to relevant certification bodies or third parties. CE/RoHS/FCC certification costs approximately RMB 6,000-10,000. China Water Conservation Certification costs approximately RMB 15,000. Certifications such as CUPC/WRAS/KIW/ACS/WATERMARK/JIS have higher costs -- generally RMB 50,000-100,000 per individual certification item -- and most certifications involve third-party annual factory audits and annual fees, which are costly. The specific cost-bearing must be determined based on customer and market circumstances. At the same time, all of the above certifications can add product models through the listing method, with a listing fee of approximately RMB 3,000-5,000 per product.

---

### Q: What is the relationship between understanding user demographics and product positioning and project development? | odm

A: We have always upheld the design principle of "user experience first" to deliver optimal design results and products to our partners. Users in different countries or regions have different usage habits, and product design details must differentiate accordingly. Different age groups and social classes have different product requirements; appearance, color, and function requirements all need differentiated design. Different application venues and usage scenarios impose different requirements on functionality, product form, and product value. Different market positioning and product positioning impose different cost requirements. During the design phase, control and consideration must be given to material selection, cost, structure, and manufacturing cost to ensure market suitability.

---

### Q: What materials need to be prepared during the project initiation process? | odm

A: Before project initiation, it is best to provide written product function descriptions and reference product design images so that development personnel can accurately understand the design requirements and intent. During the project initiation process, both parties need to sign the corresponding development cooperation agreement and confidentiality agreement and provide written confirmation of the project development requirements. After project initiation, if relevant components and supporting products have already completed structural or appearance design, the CAD source files of the relevant designs must be provided. If no relevant design files are available, 2D drawings of the key dimensions for product fit must be provided. If relevant 3D samples or market samples already exist, please provide 1-3 sets of samples as design testing or reference.

---

### Q: How is risk assessment conducted for project development? | odm

A: After confirming the development requirements for each project, we conduct rigorous evaluation and review to reduce design risks, improve first-pass design success rates, and shorten product design cycles to achieve the fastest possible time to market. First Review: After requirements confirmation, we conduct the initial requirements review to ensure the relevant requirements are achievable. Second Review: After the first review, R&D design proceeds. We decompose and complete the product electronic schematic, functional logic flowchart, and product structural design draft. Based on the draft, we perform software simulation and internal design review to ensure functional logic and structure are achievable. 3D Verification: After adjusting the design based on the design review results, we conduct 3D-printed prototype verification. Fourth Review: After adjusting the design based on the 3D assembly and functional verification, we conduct mold design verification. Sample Verification: After assembling and functionally verifying parts from the formal mold, mold repair verification is conducted. Trial Production Verification: After confirming molded parts, small-batch trial production is conducted. Through these six stages of product design and functional verification, product design risks are eliminated and design risks are reduced.

---
### Q: What is the project plan after approval? | odm

A: The project manager breaks down project tasks, phases, and scheduled timelines according to the project's functional requirements, complexity, and development cycle, assigning them to designated developers. A project schedule kanban board is created, and developers proceed with development based on the project schedule.

---

### Q: Why are 3D prototyping costs so high? | odm

A: The reasons for higher 3D prototyping costs are as follows:
Material cost: 3D printing materials are usually quite expensive, especially high-performance or specialty materials. The price of different material types varies significantly, so selecting a specific material will also affect the cost.
Equipment investment: 3D printing equipment itself requires a high capital investment and needs maintenance and upgrades. These costs are passed on to service provider charges.
Process complexity: For complex designs or prototyping needs requiring high precision, more advanced and precise 3D printing equipment may be necessary, and the operation and maintenance costs of these devices are higher.
Online and customized services: Some service providers offer convenient online services such as online order submission and fast delivery. Simultaneously, to meet personalized customer needs, customized operations may be required, and these additional conveniences and services naturally increase costs.
Lack of batch effect: Compared to traditional mass production, 3D prototyping is typically small-batch or even single-piece production and cannot enjoy the cost advantages brought by mass production.
It should be noted that with the development and popularization of 3D printing technology, some low-cost or consumer-grade 3D printing equipment has emerged, making the cost of some simple prototyping tasks relatively low.

---

### Q: Why are mold costs higher than what I know? | odm

A: The main reasons for higher mold costs are as follows:
Material cost: Molds typically use high wear-resistant, high-strength metal materials such as steel or aluminum alloy. The relatively high cost of these materials directly affects mold expenses.
Design and manufacturing complexity: Mold design and manufacturing require experienced engineers and technicians who need to consider multiple aspects such as product shape, dimensional precision, and injection molding processes. Manufacturing molds requires specialized equipment and processes, including CNC machine tools, EDM (electrical discharge machining), wire cutting, and other high-precision processes. These require significant investment in manpower and material resources, thus raising mold costs.
Trial molding and debugging: After mold manufacturing is completed, trial molding and debugging work is usually needed to ensure the mold can operate normally and produce products meeting requirements. This process consumes time and cost.
Service life and maintenance: After a certain number of production cycles, molds may need repair, replacement, or maintenance due to wear, fatigue, or other reasons. These additional maintenance costs also increase mold expenses.
Batch effect and economic scale: Generally, mold costs are amortized as production quantity increases, i.e., mass production can reduce the average mold cost.

---

### Q: How long does it take to provide sample confirmation? | odm

A: The time to provide sample confirmation varies depending on different situations. The following are some factors that may affect sample confirmation time:
Design complexity: If the product design is relatively simple and conforms to conventional standards, the sample production and confirmation time may be relatively short. However, if the product design is more complex or requires special processing techniques, the sample production and confirmation time may be relatively longer.
Mold manufacturing time: Sample confirmation requires making the mold first, and the mold manufacturing time varies depending on the mold's complexity and manufacturing process. Typically, mold manufacturing time can range from several weeks to several months.
Trial molding and debugging time: After sample production is completed, trial molding and debugging work is required to ensure the mold can operate normally and produce products meeting requirements. This process takes time, potentially ranging from a few days to several weeks.
Logistics and delivery time: If samples need to be shipped via logistics to the customer for confirmation, logistics and delivery time also need to be considered. This may be affected by factors such as geographic location, transportation method, and customer requirements.
In summary, sample confirmation time is affected by multiple factors and it is difficult to provide a specific time range. Specific evaluation should be based on actual circumstances.

---

### Q: What are the specific processes of mold processing? | odm

A: The main processes of mold processing are as follows:
Mold drawing design—Drawing review—Mold base and material preparation—Mold base processing—Mold core processing—Electrode processing—Mold part processing—Inspection—Mold assembly—Fitting—Trial molding—Production.

---

### Q: What processes are involved in mold processing? | odm

A: The main processing techniques involved in mold processing include:
Flash trimming—Rough grinding—Milling—Bench work—CNC rough machining—Heat treatment—Precision grinding—CNC finish machining—EDM (electrical discharge machining)—Slow wire EDM.

---

### Q: What components are involved in mold processing? | odm

A: The basic composition of plastic molds: mold base (the supporting part of the entire mold core), mold cavity (the flow channel for plastic material), mold core (the structure determining product formation), clamping plate (for securing the mold core), positioning guide pillars, and cooling system components.
Main parts involved in mold processing:
A/B plate (upper and lower molds)—Slider—Compression block—Insert—Gate—Mold hot runner.

---

### Q: What are the material requirements for mold bases? | odm

A: High-quality plastic mold surfaces must have sufficient hardness and excellent wear resistance. Plastic molds must have excellent machinability, so the steel hardness used must be appropriate.
To produce superior quality plastic products, the cavity surface's polishing performance must achieve excellence, minimizing surface roughness value.
Mold materials must have good thermal stability; after quenching treatment, plastic mold dimensions should essentially remain unchanged.

---

### Q: What materials are mold bases typically made of? | odm

A: Common materials for mold bases: Generally, mold bases use medium carbon steel. Domestic grades are 45# steel and 50# steel. Foreign grades are S55, S45, S50. LKM mold bases use 50# steel.

---

### Q: What software is used for mold design? What file formats? | odm

A: Common 3D design software mainly includes AutoCAD (CAD for short), Pro/Engineer (ProE for short), SolidWorks, 3D MAX, etc.
Common file formats: DWG, DXF, DWS, STP, STL, SLDPRT, MAX, etc.

---

### Q: What does "1-cavity-2" and "2-by-2" mold mean? | odm

A: The number of cavities in a mold represents the number of product parts, generally expressed as "X by Y".
For example, "1-cavity-2" means the mold produces 2 cavities of one type of product. If the mold produces 2 types of products with 2 cavities each, it is expressed as "2-by-2", and so on.

---

### Q: What are the common plastic materials used in injection molding? | odm

A: Common materials in injection molding:
Shell/appearance category: ABS, polyolefin materials (PP)
Sensor window category: PC (polycarbonate), PMMA (acrylic), PS, AS, etc.
Smart toilet cover category: polyolefin materials (PP), polyethylene types (ABS), urea-formaldehyde resin (PF)
Valve body/seat category: PPS, PPO, PPA, PA9T, etc. (modified engineering plastics and specialty engineering plastics), PA66+GF, POM (polyoxymethylene), PSU (polysulfone), etc.

---

### Q: How many cycles can a mold typically achieve in its service life? | odm

A: Mold lifespan is influenced by multiple factors. Typically, mold lifespan can be roughly categorized as follows:
1. Small injection mold lifespan generally around 50,000-100,000 cycles;
2. Large injection mold lifespan generally around 100,000-300,000 cycles;
3. Stamping mold lifespan generally exceeds 500,000 cycles;
4. Die-casting mold lifespan generally around 500,000-1,000,000 cycles;
Overall, mold lifespan is closely related to factors such as usage frequency, maintenance status, material quality, and manufacturing processes. Different molds have different lifespans in use.

---

### Q: What are the common types of molds? | odm

A: Based on purpose and forming process, molds are generally classified into metal cold stamping molds, injection molding molds, die-casting molds, forging molds, rubber hot press molding, ceramic molds, gravity casting molds, sand casting molds, etc.

---

### Q: Why is the mold processing production cycle so long? | odm

A: Injection mold cycle: 30-45 days
Faucet body gravity casting mold: 20-30 days
Zinc alloy die-casting mold: 40-60 days
Hot forging mold: 7-15 days
Mold processing involves complex processing techniques and requires high precision, so CNC precision machining and slow wire EDM are generally needed. Processing speed is slow, and after mold processing, surface treatments such as EDM are also required to ensure mold service life meets requirements. There are four main factors affecting mold delivery cycles:
1. The larger the plastic part dimensions and the more complex the structure, the longer the mold processing time.
2. Different steel materials used for molds have different production process specifications for different steel types, resulting in different time requirements.
3. The precision level of product dimensions required by the customer is also one of the factors affecting delivery time.
4. The mold lifespan and number of cavities required by the customer also cause cycle extensions or affect speed.

### Q: Quality commitment: 100% inspection of core components before shipment? | company

A: We have strict quality control procedures and comprehensive production process management. All core components undergo 100% inspection before shipment, with factory defect rate below 0.3%. Product service life exceeds 500,000 cycles, and anti-interference performance reaches 4A military-grade level, ensuring usability for over 10 years.
Circuit component full inspection process:
After SMT automatic placement, PCB undergoes fully automatic visual inspection to prevent cold solder joints;
After PCB wiring and discrete component assembly, first functional inspection is performed to ensure correct product function and mode;
After potting into sensor modules, PCB undergoes second performance inspection to ensure power consumption, distance, and other performance meets factory standards.
Battery box component full inspection process:
During battery box assembly, check for missing springs;
After battery box assembly, install batteries and test whether connection wires make proper contact.
Solenoid valve component full inspection process:
After solenoid valve assembly, perform first airtightness test to ensure air leakage is below factory standard in both outlet-sealed and solenoid-closed states;
After solenoid valve airtightness test, perform high/low pressure constant-pressure water test to ensure solenoid valve opens and closes normally under dynamic pressures of 0.9Mpa and 0.05Mpa;
Use constant-pressure water test for 3

### Q: Do you provide product quality assurance? | company

A: The company guarantees that products and accessories are shipped after passing quality inspection and can perform their superior functions under normal use and maintenance. Additionally, the company provides users with 1-year free repair and 5-year quality assurance.
1. The company provides extensive quality assurance to the product's end users. GIBO products, when proven to be properly installed and used, have a free repair warranty period of 1 year.
2. Products purchased beyond the 1-year free repair period but within the 5-year quality assurance period are guaranteed repair by the company, with costs charged at discretion. Products beyond the 5-year assurance period will have costs and related service fees charged at discretion (specific fee standards follow the company's service manual).
3. Product warranty or assurance only covers product or accessory performance and function. Product or accessory appearance and other aspects are not included in the warranty scope.
4. The following situations are not covered by warranty or assurance:
1) No sales label on the product or the date indicated on the sales label has exceeded the warranty or assurance period.
2) Quality issues arising from improper installation, replacement, repair, and similar actions.
3) Quality issues arising from misuse, abuse, negligence, and use of non-GIBO accessories.
4) Simple maintenance such as battery replacement, filter mesh cleaning, and distance adjustment.

Product type testing report: Ensure products meet relevant laws, regulations, and functional and performance requirements
Design change confirmation: Design adjustments made according to actual needs
Formal mass production and delivery cycle communication: Prepare process documentation to ensure quality control and meet customer timely delivery requirements
Other tedious development steps require no attention; we will provide you with the best products and solutions based on your clear requirements.

### Q: Is the estimated product quotation the final unit price? | company

A: Product quotations are affected by factors such as selected materials, processes, manufacturing difficulty, and batch size. In the early design stages, a reference product quotation is generally provided. The final unit price is typically finalized during the formal sample and trial production stages, with final quoting based on order quantity and product materials, processes, and processing costs.
Generally, based on our long-term experience, estimated product quotations and final transaction unit prices have an up-and-down variance of 10-20%.

---

### Q: How is the batch product unit price determined? | company

A: Generally, product unit price calculation consists of: material cost + auxiliary material cost + transportation cost 1% + wastage 1% + manufacturing cost 8% + management cost 5% + design cost allocation + tax 13% + profit 5-15%.
Factors affecting product pricing mainly include order quantity and processing costs. The recommended economic production quantity for initial orders is generally 3-5,000 units.

---
